441: # ---- management: ether1 addressing and its own routing table ----------------
442: # ether1 gets the container's management addresses, and their return path lives
443: # in a `mgmt` routing table selected by a rule on those source addresses: a
444: # default route the lab adds to `main` (a static 0.0.0.0/0, a PPPoE client's
445: # default route, a dual-WAN pair) would otherwise take SSH/REST replies away
446: # from the management network while the data plane works. CHR's default ether1
447: # DHCP client is disabled: the address is static. Everything is tagged
448: # clab-mgmt and re-applied on every boot from the container's CURRENT addressing
449: # (a restart can bring a new IP), while the bootstrap channel itself
450: # (link-local) never depends on it.
451: mgmt_fail() {
452:     log "management setup FAILED: $1 (redeploy the node: containerlab deploy --reconfigure)"
453:     wait "$QEMU_PID"
454:     exit 0
455: }
456: rest_has() { # rest_has <path?query>: the GET answered a non-empty JSON array
457:     case "$(rest "$AUTH" "$1")" in '['*'{'*) return 0 ;; *) return 1 ;; esac
458: }
459: rest_add() { # rest_add <what> <path> <json>: a 2xx with no error object, else mgmt_fail with the router's detail
460:     _r="$(rest_raw "$AUTH" "$2" "$3" 20)"
461:     case "$_r" in
462:         2??\ *) is_err "$_r" && mgmt_fail "$1: ${_r#* }" ;;
463:         *) mgmt_fail "$1: ${_r:-no response}" ;;
464:     esac
465: }
466: rest_count() { # rest_count <path?query>: number of objects the GET answered
467:     rest "$AUTH" "$1" | tr '{' '\n' | grep -c '"\.id"'
468: }
469: rest_clear() { # rest_clear <menu>: remove its clab-mgmt objects
470:     _ids="$(rest "$AUTH" "$1?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT" | tr '{' '\n' \
471:         | sed -n 's/.*"\.id":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' | paste -sd, -)"
472:     [ -z "$_ids" ] || rest_add "remove old $1" "$1/remove" "{\".id\":\"$_ids\"}"
473: }
474: # The tagged objects already match the container's addressing (a restart with
475: # the same IP): leave them alone, re-applying would only drop live sessions.
476: mgmt_in_place() {
477:     _want=0
478:     if [ -n "$M4" ]; then
479:         rest_has "ip/address?interface=ether1&address=$M4&comment=$MGMT_COMMENT" || return 1
480:         rest_has "routing/rule?src-address=${M4%/*}/32&table=$MGMT_TABLE" || return 1
481:         [ -z "$G4" ] || rest_has "ip/route?routing-table=$MGMT_TABLE&dst-address=0.0.0.0/0&gateway=$G4" || return 1
482:         _want=$((_want + 1))
483:     fi
484:     if [ -n "$M6" ]; then
485:         rest_has "ipv6/address?interface=ether1&address=$M6&comment=$MGMT_COMMENT" || return 1
486:         _want=$((_want + 1))
487:     fi
488:     [ "$(rest_count "ip/address?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT")$(rest_count "ipv6/address?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT")" \
489:         = "$([ -n "$M4" ] && echo 1 || echo 0)$([ -n "$M6" ] && echo 1 || echo 0)" ] || return 1
490:     rest_has "ipv6/nd?interface=ether1&ra-lifetime=none" || return 1
491:     [ "$_want" -gt 0 ]
492: }
493: mgmt_setup() {
494:     if mgmt_in_place; then
495:         log "management on ether1 unchanged: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6}"
496:         return 0
497:     fi
498:     for _m in routing/rule ip/route ipv6/route ip/address ipv6/address; do rest_clear "$_m"; done
499:     rest_has "routing/table?name=$MGMT_TABLE" \
500:         || rest_add "routing table $MGMT_TABLE" routing/table/add \
501:             "{\"name\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",\"fib\":\"yes\",\"comment\":\"$MGMT_COMMENT\"}"
502:     _c="\"comment\":\"$MGMT_COMMENT\""
503:     if [ -n "$M4" ]; then
504:         _net4="$(ipcalc -n "$M4" | sed -n 's/^NETWORK=//p')/${M4#*/}"
505:         rest_add "ether1 address $M4" ip/address/add "{\"address\":\"$M4\",\"interface\":\"ether1\",$_c}"
506:         rest_add "route $_net4 in table $MGMT_TABLE" ip/route/add \
507:             "{\"dst-address\":\"$_net4\",\"gateway\":\"ether1\",\"routing-table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
508:         [ -z "$G4" ] || rest_add "default via $G4 in table $MGMT_TABLE" ip/route/add \
509:             "{\"dst-address\":\"0.0.0.0/0\",\"gateway\":\"$G4\",\"routing-table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
510:         rest_add "routing rule for ${M4%/*}" routing/rule/add \
511:             "{\"src-address\":\"${M4%/*}/32\",\"action\":\"lookup-only-in-table\",\"table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
512:     fi
513:     if [ -n "$M6" ]; then
514:         rest_add "ether1 address $M6" ipv6/address/add \
515:             "{\"address\":\"$M6\",\"interface\":\"ether1\",\"advertise\":\"no\",$_c}"
516:         # RouterOS normalizes the host bits of dst-address (::3/64 -> ::/64)
517:         rest_add "IPv6 route $M6 in table $MGMT_TABLE" ipv6/route/add \
518:             "{\"dst-address\":\"$M6\",\"gateway\":\"ether1\",\"routing-table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
519:         [ -z "$G6" ] || rest_add "IPv6 default via $G6 in table $MGMT_TABLE" ipv6/route/add \
520:             "{\"dst-address\":\"::/0\",\"gateway\":\"$G6%ether1\",\"routing-table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
521:         rest_add "routing rule for ${M6%/*}" routing/rule/add \
522:             "{\"src-address\":\"${M6%/*}/128\",\"action\":\"lookup-only-in-table\",\"table\":\"$MGMT_TABLE\",$_c}"
523:     fi
524:     _id="$(rest "$AUTH" "ip/dhcp-client?interface=ether1" | sed -n 's/.*"\.id":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' | head -n1)"
525:     [ -z "$_id" ] || rest_add "disable the ether1 DHCP client" ip/dhcp-client/set \
526:         "{\"numbers\":\"$_id\",\"disabled\":\"yes\"}"
527:     # RouterOS's default ND entry (interface=all, ra-lifetime=30m) sends router
528:     # advertisements on ether1 too, and every host on the management network
529:     # with accept_ra would install the router as its IPv6 default route. An
530:     # ether1 entry with ra-lifetime=none keeps the RAs harmless and withdraws a
531:     # route a host already learned (a disabled entry does not override "all").
532:     # /ipv6/nd keeps no comment, so its entry is found by interface.
533:     _nd="$(rest "$AUTH" "ipv6/nd?interface=ether1" | sed -n 's/.*"\.id":"\([^"]*\)".*/\1/p' | head -n1)"
534:     if [ -n "$_nd" ]; then
535:         rest_add "ether1 router advertisements off" ipv6/nd/set \
536:             "{\"numbers\":\"$_nd\",\"ra-lifetime\":\"none\",\"disabled\":\"no\"}"
537:     else
538:         rest_add "ether1 router advertisements off" ipv6/nd/add \
539:             "{\"interface\":\"ether1\",\"ra-lifetime\":\"none\"}"
540:     fi
541:     # Prove they took (a transport flake answers an empty body, which is not
542:     # an error by the set-contract).
543:     if [ -n "$M4" ]; then

545:         rest_has "routing/rule?src-address=${M4%/*}/32&table=$MGMT_TABLE" \
546:             || mgmt_fail "routing rule for ${M4%/*} not applied"
547:     fi
548:     log "management on ether1: ${M4:-no IPv4}${M6:+, $M6}, return path in routing table $MGMT_TABLE"
549: }
550: # Keep discovery on static data interfaces, excluding management. Set this only
551: # on first boot, before attaching eth0: startup-config and subsequent user changes
552: # can select a different list, and restarts must preserve that choice.
553: discovery_setup() {
554:     rest_add "management interface list" interface/list/add \
555:         "{\"name\":\"clab-management\",\"comment\":\"$MGMT_COMMENT\"}"
556:     rest_add "management interface list member" interface/list/member/add \
557:         "{\"list\":\"clab-management\",\"interface\":\"ether1\",\"comment\":\"$MGMT_COMMENT\"}"
558:     rest_add "discovery interface list" interface/list/add \
559:         "{\"name\":\"clab-discovery\",\"include\":\"static\",\"exclude\":\"clab-management\",\"comment\":\"$MGMT_COMMENT\"}"

566:     rest_has "interface/list?name=clab-discovery&include=static&exclude=clab-management&comment=$MGMT_COMMENT" \

569:         *'"discover-interface-list":"clab-discovery"'*) : ;;
570:         *) mgmt_fail "management discovery policy not applied" ;;
571:     esac
572:     log "neighbor discovery: static interfaces except ether1 (clab-discovery)"
573: }
574: # Join the management network, then have the router reach its gateway from the
575: # management address: proves the return path both ways, and the ARP/ND request
576: # refreshes the gateway's neighbor entry when the IP was used by another MAC
577: # before (a redeployed lab hands out the same addresses).
578: mgmt_attach() {
579:     [ -e "$SYSNET/$MGMT_BR/brif/eth0" ] || brctl addif "$MGMT_BR" eth0
580:     ip link set eth0 up
581:     for _pair in "$G4 ${M4%/*}" "$G6 ${M6%/*}"; do
582:         # shellcheck disable=SC2086  # split "gateway source"; an empty half skips the pair
583:         set -- $_pair
584:         [ $# -eq 2 ] || continue
585:         _t0=$(date +%s)
586:         until case "$(rest "$AUTH" ping "{\"address\":\"$1\",\"src-address\":\"$2\",\"count\":\"2\"}")" in
587:                 *'"received":"'[1-9]*) true ;; *) false ;; esac; do
588:             if [ $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 )) -ge 30 ]; then
589:                 log "gateway $1 does not answer from $2 after $(( $(date +%s) - _t0 ))s (continuing)"
590:                 break
591:             fi
592:             sleep 1
593:         done
594:     done
595: }

597: # Bootstrap + startup-config import happen ONCE per container lifetime. On a
598: # restart the CHR disk keeps its config and the marker (in the container rw
599: # layer) survives, so the bootstrap (which could wait forever if the config
600: # disabled `www` or changed the account, or reject duplicate `add` lines) is
601: # skipped. A fresh deploy recreates the container and the disk, so the marker
602: # and the config are both gone and the bootstrap runs.
603: if [ -f "$DONE_MARKER" ]; then
604:     log "already bootstrapped (restart): skipping config"
605:     mgmt_setup
606:     mgmt_attach
607:     ready
608:     wait "$QEMU_PID"
609:     exit 0

612: # ---- credentials + identity -----------------------------------------------
613: # Every credential-establishing call is CHECKED: the ready marker never
614: # advertises credentials that do not work.
615: FACTORY="admin:"
616: rest "$FACTORY" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1 || FACTORY="admin:$PASSWORD"
617: if [ "$USERNAME" = "admin" ]; then
618:     if [ -n "$PASSWORD" ]; then
619:         out="$(rest "$FACTORY" user/set \
620:             "{\"numbers\":\"admin\",\"password\":\"$(json_str "$PASSWORD")\"}")"
621:         is_err "$out" && bootstrap_fail "set admin password: ${out:-no response}"
622:     fi
623:     AUTH="admin:$PASSWORD"
624: else
625:     out="$(rest "$FACTORY" user/add \
626:         "{\"name\":\"$(json_str "$USERNAME")\",\"password\":\"$(json_str "$PASSWORD")\",\"group\":\"full\"}")"
627:     is_err "$out" && bootstrap_fail "create user $USERNAME: ${out:-no response}"
628:     AUTH="$USERNAME:$PASSWORD"
629:     # The factory `admin` still has a BLANK password and is reachable over the
630:     # forwarded services: disable it so the node keeps no unauthenticated
631:     # full-access account.
632:     out="$(rest "$AUTH" user/set "{\"numbers\":\"admin\",\"disabled\":\"yes\"}")"
633:     is_err "$out" && bootstrap_fail "disable factory admin: ${out:-no response}"
634:     if rest "admin:" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1; then
635:         bootstrap_fail "factory admin still authenticates after disable"
636:     fi
637: fi
638: rest "$AUTH" system/resource >/dev/null 2>&1 \
639:     || bootstrap_fail "the requested credentials do not authenticate"
640: out="$(rest "$AUTH" system/identity/set "{\"name\":\"$(json_str "$HOSTNAME_ARG")\"}")"
641: is_err "$out" && bootstrap_fail "set identity: ${out:-no response}"
642: got="$(rest "$AUTH" system/identity)"
643: case "$got" in
644:     *"\"name\":\"$HOSTNAME_ARG\""*) : ;;
645:     *) bootstrap_fail "identity not applied: ${got:-no response}" ;;
646: esac

652: # ---- startup config -------------------------------------------------------
653: if [ -s "$STARTUP_CFG" ]; then
654:     bridge_ll
655:     # one-shot HTTP server on the bridge's link-local address; the router
656:     # fetches from it through ether1 (fetch takes a zoned link-local address

674:                     ;;
675:                 *)

*) return 1 ;; esac\n}\nrest_add() { # rest_add <what> <path> <json>: a 2xx with no error object, else mgmt_fail with the router's detail\n    _r=\"$(rest_raw \"$AUTH\" \"$2\" \"$3\" 20)\"\n    case \"$_r\" in\n        2??\\ *) is_err \"$_r\" && mgmt_fail \"$1: ${_r#* }\" ;;\n        *) mgmt_fail \"$1: ${_r:-no response}\" ;;\n    esac\n}\nrest_count() { # rest_count <path?query>: number of objects the GET answered\n    rest \"$AUTH\" \"$1\" | tr '{' '\\n' | grep -c '\"\\.id\"'\n}\nrest_clear() { # rest_clear <menu>: remove its clab-mgmt objects\n    _ids=\"$(rest \"$AUTH\" \"$1?comment=$MGMT_COMMENT\" | tr '{' '\\n' \\\n        | sed -n 's/.*\"\\.id\":\"\\([^\"]*\\)\".*/\\1/p' | paste -sd, -)\"\n    [ -z \"$_ids\" ] || rest_add \"remove old $1\" \"$1/remove\" \"{\\\".id\\\":\\\"$_ids\\\"}\"\n}\n# The tagged objects already match the container's addressing (a restart with\n# the same IP): leave them alone, re-applying would only drop live sessions.\nmgmt_in_place() {\n    _want=0\n    if [ -n \"$M4\" ]; 1. `discovery_setup` is called only on first boot (after the DONE_MARKER check). But there's a subtlety: `mgmt_setup` runs on restart too, and re-applies `clab-mgmt` objects. On restart, `discovery_setup` is not run — good, per doc "Restarts preserve the selected policy".

However, there's an issue: on first boot, `mgmt_setup` clears clab-mgmt objects from `routing/rule ip/route ipv6/route ip/address ipv6/address` but not from `interface/list` or `interface/list/member`. On redeploy (--reconfigure) the container is fresh so no issue. 4. Now the bigger correctness questions for `discovery_setup`:

a) It runs on first boot only. But consider the doc statement: "Restarts preserve the selected policy, including on nodes created before this default was introduced." For existing nodes (pre-existing containers with DONE_MARKER), discovery_setup never runs → they keep the old behavior (default discovery). OK, that's intentional.

b) `rest_add` for `interface/list/add` — if a user's startup config... no, config is imported after.

c) What about `mgmt_setup` being run on RESTART and its `rest_clear` of menus not including interface lists — but on restart discovery_setup isn't called. However, consider a restart where mgmt addressing changed: mgmt_setup re-applies. Interface lists remain. Fine.

d) A real question: on first boot, discovery_setup runs BEFORE mgmt_attach, i.e., before eth0 joins the bridge. The discovery-settings `set` with `discover-interface-list=clab-discovery` where clab-discovery includes static interfaces excluding clab-management. ether1 is in clab-management. ether2..N are static → discovery on. Good.

e) Potential issue: `rest_has "interface/list?name=clab-discovery&include=static&exclude=clab-management..."` — does RouterOS REST filter support `include`/`exclude` params on interface/list? Probably. But `exclude=clab-management` — the value contains a hyphen, fine. But what about URL-encoding? Values are simple. OK.

f) Wait — an important issue: `discovery_setup` is inside first-boot path AFTER the DONE_MARKER check. But the doc says "Startup configuration is imported after this default is applied." Yes.

g) Now, there's a subtle ordering bug: On first boot, `discovery_setup` runs before `mgmt_attach`. mgmt_attach brings eth0 into the bridge, and neighbor discovery from OTHER nodes can now see this router on data ports only. Fine.

h) What about `ip/neighbor/discovery-settings/set` — the RouterOS REST endpoint for discovery settings. In RouterOS REST API, `/rest/ip/neighbor/discovery-settings/set` with JSON body. The response for a `set` is empty body. rest_raw returns "200 " with empty body → passes is_err (no error object). Then read-back check via GET. OK.

5. Test file changes. The first added test: "Ensure discovery uses data ports and excludes management by default" — checks `/ip neighbor print count-only where interface=ether1` = 0 and ether2 = 1. This relies on LLDP from peer r2 over the data link. r1 and r2 connected via ether2 presumably. Let me check the test topology in the robot file.

Also: does this test run BEFORE r3's test? r3 has a rejected startup config and never becomes healthy. The new test is added after the r2 SSH test and before r3 test. Discovery via MNDP/LLDP: RouterOS neighbor discovery announces via MNDP (UDP 5678) and LLDP. Wait — the docs previously said "LLDP/MNDP work on the management network" and the change removed that phrase from the management section since discovery is now disabled on ether1. The header comment of chr-launch.sh says data bridges forward LLDP.

Potential flakiness: neighbor entries might persist from earlier (ether1 neighbor entries created before the change? No, fresh deploy). Timing: neighbors appear within a minute (MNDP every 60s?). Wait Until Keyword Succeeds 90s 5s — should be OK.

But there's an issue: `/ip neighbor print count-only where interface=ether2` == 1 — requires exactly one neighbor on ether2. In the topology, r1-r2 are connected via ether2 each. But if topology has more links... Let me read the test file and its topology (maybe embedded in the robot via lab-file-name variable pointing to a .yaml file — is that yaml in the snapshot? Because the r1 test (explicit static) does the set and save on r1 later. Contains `discover-interface-list=clab-discovery` → asserted. Good.

Reasoning
Topology: r1-r2 via ether2/ether2. r3 has no data links. So the "Discovery uses data port" check: ether2 exactly 1 neighbor (peer), ether1 zero. But r3 exists with discovery enabled on ether2 (no link though — r3 has no data links, and r3 is on the same management bridge but its ether1 is excluded). r3's MNDP on ether2 goes nowhere. But r1's ether2 neighbor count: could r3's MNDP arrive over the mgmt bridge? No, r3 has no link to r1's ether2.

But wait: r3's ether1 broadcasts MNDP onto the management bridge (until discovery_setup turns it off on r3's first boot... r3's startup config import FAILS, but discovery_setup runs before import, so r3's ether1 is in clab-management, excluded from discovery. Good.

Hmm, but another wrinkle: neighbor entries on ether1 could exist from BEFORE discovery_setup applied (between boot and discovery_setup, RouterOS with default discovery on ether1 (or LAN list including all interfaces) may have learned the mgmt bridge's MAC or other routers' ether1 MACs). Does setting discover-interface-list=clab-discovery remove existing neighbor entries? No! `/ip neighbor` entries persist (they time out after a while — MNDP entries refresh every 60s and expire after some minutes if not refreshed). The new test "Discovery uses data port" runs `Wait Until Keyword Succeeds 90s 5s` and checks ether1 count == 0. On first boot, discovery_setup runs early (before mgmt_attach), so the window where ether1 discovery is active is short — but during that window, the router could learn the mgmt bridge's link-local MAC (52:54:01:xx) or other CHR routers sharing the mgmt bridge. Neighbor entries from MNDP expire after ~3 minutes of no refresh. LLDP entries expire too. The test waits up to 90s. Probably fine, but the neighbor entry learned during bootstrap could persist. This is a test-flakiness consideration, and the test does have a retry window. Not a definite defect.

Also, restart behavior: on restart (DONE_MARKER exists), discovery_setup is skipped. The policy persists on the disk. Good.

Now check the test suite ordering: In robot, tests run in order defined. New test "Ensure discovery uses data ports and excludes management by default" — where in the file does it appear relative to "Save the configuration..." etc.? Let me read the full robot file. Also, the "Ensure an explicit factory discovery policy survives save and clean redeploy" test destroys and redeploys the lab while later tests... let me see the order and what follows. The diff shows the redeploy test inserted after "Save the configuration..." test. What comes after? Let me read the full head robot file and base for comparison.

724: // LoadStartupConfigFileVr templates a startup-config using the file specified for VM-based nodes in
725: // the topo
726: // and puts the resulting config file by the LabDir/configDirName/startupCfgFName path.
727: func LoadStartupConfigFileVr(node Node, configDirName, startupCfgFName string) error {
728: 	nodeCfg := node.Config()
729: 	// create config directory that will be bind mounted to vrnetlab container at / path
730: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(path.Join(nodeCfg.LabDir, configDirName),
731: 		clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)

733: 	if nodeCfg.StartupConfig != "" {
734: 		// dstCfg is a path to a file on the clab host that will have rendered configuration

736: 
737: 		c, err := os.ReadFile(nodeCfg.StartupConfig)
738: 		if err != nil {
739: 			return err
740: 		}
741:

743:

745: 		if err != nil {

Reasoning
OK so plain `deploy` (non-reconfigure) does NOT remove the lab dir. But wait — how does the deploy path work after destroy? destroy removes the containers; deploy then sees no running nodes and calls `deploy` normally. The lab dir (with the saved config.auto.rsc) survives plain destroy. Then on deploy, `prepareDeployArtifacts` → creates nodes → for mikrotik_chr, PreDeploy writes the launcher, but who copies r1.rsc to config.auto.rsc?

mikrotik_chr's PreDeploy only writes the launcher. But the topology has `startup-config: r1.rsc`. Where does the copy happen for this kind? Maybe in `prepareDeployArtifacts` there's a generic step calling node's PreDeploy... Let me check how startup config is resolved for this kind. The `mikrotik_chr` docs say "The file is copied to the node's lab directory as ftpboot/config.auto.rsc". Let's grep for how mikrotik_chr gets its startup config. Check nodes/mikrotik_chr for LoadStartupConfigFileVr.

73: // AddEndpoint override version maps the endpoint name to an ethX-based name before adding it to the
74: // node endpoints. Returns an error if the mapping goes wrong.
75: func (vr *VRNode) AddEndpoint(e clablinks.Endpoint) error {
76: 	endpointName := e.GetIfaceName()
77: 	// Slightly modified check: if it doesn't match the VMInterfaceRegexp, pass it to
78: 	// GetMappedInterfaceName. If it fails, then the interface name is wrong.
79: 	if vr.InterfaceRegexp != nil && !(VMInterfaceRegexp.MatchString(endpointName)) {
80: 		mappedName, err := vr.OverwriteNode.GetMappedInterfaceName(endpointName)
81: 		if err != nil {
82: 			return fmt.Errorf(
83: 				"%q interface name %q could not be mapped to an ethX-based interface name: %w",
84: 				vr.Cfg.ShortName,
85: 				e.GetIfaceName(),
86: 				err,
87: 			)
88: 		}
89: 		log.Debugf(
90: 			"Interface Mapping: Mapping interface %q (ifAlias) to %q (ifName)",
91: 			endpointName,
92: 			mappedName,
93: 		)
94: 		e.SetIfaceName(mappedName)
95: 		e.SetIfaceAlias(endpointName)
96: 	}
97: 
98: 	if e.GetNode() == nil {
99: 		e.SetNode(vr)
100: 	}
101: 
102: 	vr.Endpoints = append(vr.Endpoints, e)
103: 	return nil
104: }

162: // container.

164: 	preStopPrepareVrnetlabQcowAlias(ctx, &vr.DefaultNode)

168: func preStopPrepareVrnetlabQcowAlias(ctx context.Context, d *DefaultNode) {
169: 	aliasName, ok := vrnetlabQcowAliasName(d.Config().Image)
170: 	if !ok {
171: 		log.Debugf(
172: 			"node %q pre-stop vrnetlab qcow alias skipped: unable to infer tag from image %q",
173: 			d.Config().ShortName,
174: 			d.Config().Image,
175: 		)
176: 		return
177: 	}
178: 
179: 	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, 10*time.Second)
180: 	defer cancel()
181: 
182: 	// Some vrnetlab nodes rename the original versioned qcow image after first boot and fail on
183: 	// subsequent starts when they try to rediscover a versioned qcow filename. If there is exactly
184: 	// one non-overlay qcow file in / and our alias is absent, create a hardlink alias based on the
185: 	// image tag.
186: 	cmd := fmt.Sprintf(
187: 		`alias="/%s"; `+
188: 			`[ -e "$alias" ] && exit 0; `+
189: 			`src=""; `+
190: 			`if [ -f /sros.qcow2 ] && [ "/sros.qcow2" != "$alias" ]; then `+
191: 			`src="/sros.qcow2"; `+
192: 			`else `+
193: 			`set -- /*.qcow2; `+
194: 			`if [ "$1" != "/*.qcow2" ]; then `+
195: 			`for f in "$@"; do `+
196: 			`[ "$f" = "$alias" ] && continue; `+
197: 			`base="${f##*/}"; `+
198: 			`case "$base" in *overlay*.qcow2) continue ;; esac; `+
199: 			`if [ -n "$src" ]; then src=""; break; fi; `+
200: 			`src="$f"; `+
201: 			`done; `+
202: 			`fi; `+
203: 			`fi; `+
204: 			`[ -n "$src" ] || exit 0; `+
205: 			`ln "$src" "$alias"`,
206: 		aliasName,
207: 	)
208: 
209: 	execCmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice([]string{"sh", "-lc", cmd})
210: 	res, err := d.RunExec(ctx, execCmd)
211: 	if err != nil {
212: 		log.Warnf(
213: 			"node %q pre-stop vrnetlab qcow alias preparation failed: %v",
214: 			d.Config().ShortName,
215: 			err,
216: 		)
217: 		return
218: 	}
219: 
220: 	if res != nil && res.ReturnCode != 0 {
221: 		log.Warnf(
222: 			"node %q pre-stop vrnetlab qcow alias prep returned code %d (stderr: %s)",
223: 			d.Config().ShortName,
224: 			res.ReturnCode,
225: 			res.Stderr,
226: 		)
227: 	}
228: }
229: 
230: func vrnetlabQcowAliasName(image string) (string, bool) {
231: 	tag, ok := imageTag(image)
232: 	if !ok {
233: 		return "", false
234: 	}
235: 
236: 	return "clab-" + tag + ".qcow2", true
237: }
238: 
239: func imageTag(image string) (string, bool) {
240: 	if at := strings.LastIndex(image, "@"); at != -1 {
241: 		image = image[:at]
242: 	}
243: 
244: 	lastSlash := strings.LastIndex(image, "/")
245: 	lastColon := strings.LastIndex(image, ":")
246: 	if lastColon == -1 || lastColon < lastSlash {
247: 		return "", false
248: 	}
249: 
250: 	tag := image[lastColon+1:]
251: 	if tag == "" {
252: 		return "", false
253: 	}
254: 
255: 	if !imageTagRE.MatchString(tag) {
256: 		return "", false
257: 	}
258: 
259: 	return tag, true
260: }
